Rockstar Moves Up GTA 6 Pre-Load Date, Confirming Massive File Size

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rockstar has brought Grand Theft Auto 6's pre-load date forward by two days and finally confirmed how much storage we'll need. According to a new Newswire announcement, pre-loading begins on November 10 instead of November 12, and the file could require up to 212 GB of space, depending on the device.

According to a new rumor, Rockstar Games could release the GTA 6 PC port just a few months after the highly anticipated console launch.

With the game still scheduled to launch on November 19, that gives you a little more time to get the download out of the way. The earlier start is welcome news for anyone with a slower internet connection, although finding enough free storage might be the bigger problem.

In its Newswire announcement, Rockstar says the pre-load file will require "up to 212 GB depending on your device." The developer also advises everyone to clear enough room before pre-loading begins. That's a substantial amount of storage, but the wording is worth paying attention to.

The phrase "up to" suggests that 212 GB is an upper limit rather than a fixed requirement for every console. Rockstar hasn't provided a platform-by-platform breakdown, and we still don't know whether the final installed game will take up exactly the same amount of space.

The figure is close to the 210 GB size reported on October 27 by PlayStation Game Size, an account that tracks information from the PlayStation Store. That earlier estimate concerned the PS5 version and wasn't an official confirmation from Rockstar. We now have a confirmed upper limit for pre-loading, although the final installation size remains unclear.

We now have a confirmed upper limit for pre-loading, although the final installation size remains unclear.

The distinction matters if you're trying to decide how many games to uninstall. The amount of storage required for pre-loading and the game's eventual installed footprint can differ, especially with updates around launch. For now, making at least 212 GB available, preferably with some extra room, seems like a sensible precaution.

I can already see this becoming a problem for me. My drives are usually left with just 10–20 GB of free space, mostly because I refuse to uninstall games I might suddenly get hyperfixated on again. I always tell myself I'll return to them someday, even if that means keeping them installed for months. If that sounds familiar, you might want to start clearing some space now.

Rockstar originally announced November 12 as the pre-load date, giving everyone a week to download the game before release. Its latest Newswire post moves that forward to November 10, with slightly different timings for each platform.

On PlayStation 5, digital pre-loading begins at midnight local time on November 10. Xbox Series X|S pre-loading, meanwhile, begins globally at 12 a.m. GMT on the same day. That gives console owners several days to finish downloading what could be one of the largest games in their libraries.

Rockstar's information hasn't been entirely consistent, however. Its online store listing still mentions November 12, while an older section of its customer support article also retains that date. The same support article now includes the updated November 10 timings further down, matching the latest Newswire announcement. For anyone planning their download, those newer instructions are the ones to follow.

The change also raises questions for anyone buying a physical copy. Rockstar previously confirmed that boxed editions of GTA 6 will contain a download code rather than a game disc, with retailers expected to begin distributing them on November 12. That schedule remains unchanged in the latest announcement, so anyone waiting for a boxed copy will still need to receive and redeem their code before downloading the game.

Pre-loading won't unlock GTA 6 early, of course. The game is still scheduled for November 19 on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S. But after years of waiting, having the download finished ahead of release is one less thing to worry about. I'd certainly rather spend launch day playing GTA 6 than staring at a progress bar.

South Park has aired real GTA 6 gameplay supplied by Rockstar, showing a crowded Vice City beach and Jason using a bag of dog poop to attack an NPC.

How to choose the right gaming monitor for you

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Refresh rates, size and screen technology all play a part.

Choosing the right gaming monitor is a lot like choosing the right outfit. You wouldn't wear the same thing to the gym as you would to the opera, while lifting weights in a tuxedo would inconvenience you and damage the tux. Similarly, a monitor that works great for one PC or console isn't necessarily the best fit for another and gamers who enjoy competitive shooters should look for different options than those who enjoy visually rich single-person narratives.

Only you can figure out exactly which monitor you should buy. To do that, you'll first need to understand which specs best suit your needs. Modern monitors have a lot more to them than resolution and frame rate. Different display panel types suit different uses and picture quality can vary between models. A quick crash course on the basics will get you ready to shop for the monitor you'll be happiest with in the long run.

Before we dive into specs, the first thing to consider when picking a gaming monitor is the existing hardware you'll pair it with. If your PC is decked out with an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090 or 5080, you can run games on the bleeding edge of performance and have real reasons to consider a top-shelf monitor with a 4K panel and an ultra-high refresh rate. On the other hand, if you're plugging the monitor into an older or lower-powered GPU, consider the performance you'll actually get in the games you play. If you aren't routinely seeing frame rates above, say, 90 fps, there's no reason to invest in a 240Hz monitor. Resolution and refresh rate move in opposite directions, so if you play competitive shooters and esports titles like Valorant, it's best to sacrifice pixels for higher frames, whereas players of visually rich first-person titles like Control: Resonant can make the opposite trade-off or find a balance in the middle.

Once you know the spec range you're looking for, you can move on to other concerns such as panel type, HDR and brightness support, port selection and VRR technologies like G-Sync. Size and resolution are personal preferences. Resolution is tied to graphical performance, but there are reasons other than gaming to consider a 1440p or 4K display. If you do a lot of photo or video editing, or if you watch a lot of video, a higher resolution can be worthwhile. Size is related to resolution, in the sense that the smaller of two monitors with the same resolution will have a higher pixel density  — a finer level of image detail, which can be measured in pixels per inch (PPI).

Esports players often prefer smaller monitors around 24 inches in order to see everything at once, while single-player gamers who value immersion may gravitate toward 27 inches or higher. Larger displays also support multitasking better, but work best when you sit farther back. You'll also need to consider your ideal aspect ratio. A squatter shape is better for productivity tasks, but 16:9 widescreen is the de facto aspect ratio for gaming. Some gamers enjoy ultra-widescreen monitors to fill their peripheral vision, but in games where HUD information is crucial, ultrawide displays can force you to look away from the action.

Refresh rate is often considered the most essential specification when choosing a monitor, and should be the first thing you settle on. As noted above, your GPU should dictate this. Monitors are now shipping with up to 1,000 Hz in lower-resolution modes, at which point you'd question whether you can see the difference even if you could push your hardware that far.

The average PC gamer in September 2026 was running an RTX 5070, 5060, 4060 or even a 3060, according to the Steam Hardware Survey. Accordingly, the sweet spot for the average gamer is likely to land somewhere from 144Hz to 240Hz on a 1440p or 1080p panel. If you have a similar configuration but want to play competitive, lightweight games, you may want to look for a 1080p panel with a significantly higher frame rate. Players of these titles with top-end hardware (such as an RTX 5080 or 5090) can look for 1440p or 4K panels at any refresh rate.

That said, only you can determine the real-world frame rates you're getting on your hardware. One way to do that is to turn on the Steam, NVIDIA, or Xbox in-game overlays while playing and enable the small heads-up frame rate counter they offer. You can also use a benchmarking tool like 3DMark and some games, including Cyberpunk 2077, have built-in benchmark tests. Even if your current monitor can't reach high frame rates, you'll still see a readout showing how many frames per second your graphics card is producing.

After playing multiple games that are representative of your general interests, note where you saw the highest frame rates. Unless you plan to upgrade your GPU in the next several years, there's no need to overspend on a monitor with an unachievably high refresh rate for your system.

If you're looking for a monitor to use with a console, it's worth noting that even the best current-generation consoles like the PlayStation 5 Pro or Xbox Series X don't exceed refresh rates of 120 Hz. That means you're better off putting your money toward a larger or higher-resolution monitor instead of buying a panel with blistering speeds you'll never utilize.

After determining the refresh rate, resolution and aspect ratio ideal to your setup, the biggest choice you'll make is the type of panel your monitor is fitted with. For most gamers, the two best choices are OLED and Fast IPS, in that order.

OLED is well known for its technically infinite contrast ratios, accurate colors and near-instant response times. Because each pixel is self-emissive, it produces its own light rather than relying on a backlight; OLED pixels simply do not illuminate where an image is truly black, meaning they stay black rather than turning dark grey in the way LCD panels do. That makes them ideal for gaming, especially if you value HDR performance.

However, OLED displays have their drawbacks. They require more care to prevent OLED burn-in, a condition in which leaving a static image on screen for too long leaves a permanent ghost of that image on the screen. Newer OLEDs address this with pixel-shifting that slightly moves the picture around and pixel-cleaning cycles that help keep wear and tear even across the display. If you're the sort of person who leaves your monitor on for hours when you're not using it, an OLED will force you to change that behavior.

OLEDs are also dimmer than LCDs and are consequently worse in well-lit environments. Some manufacturers offer matte coatings on their OLED models, which can help, but these displays look best in a dim or dark room. Text fringing is another issue, as is motion flicker and while the latest generation of OLED has come a long way in minimizing them, gamers who also work with documents and spreadsheets should take those issues into consideration before paying the higher cost for an OLED. Thatcost is perhaps the biggest downside, as even entry-level OLEDs start around $400 in late 2026. If you do choose to go OLED, scout for sales.

LCD panels lack the sheer wow factor of OLED, but they are more versatile. There are a range of LCD types, but Fast IPS is generally the best for gaming. IPS panels deliver excellent colors, great viewing angles and they have very consistent performance. However, regular IPS can introduce significant motion blur, which is reduced in a Fast IPS panel. For users who game in bright environments, plan to do productivity work on their gaming PC, or are on a budget, Fast IPS is an easy recommendation.

Gaming on a PC can be uneven, and one of the most frustrating issues you'll commonly encounter is screen tearing. This occurs when the GPU cranks out frames at a different rate than the monitor shows them, so the display shows parts of two frames at the same time. Substantial tearing can completely ruin a gaming session, but there's a surefire fix for screen tearing: variable refresh rate (VRR) technology. NVIDIA G-Sync, AMD FreeSync and the generic Adaptive Sync are all examples of VRR.

For a deeper dive, check out our article on screen tearing and how to fix it, but the bottom line is that you'll want G-Sync or FreeSync on your monitor. By now, it's an industry standard and you won't need to spend much extra money, if any, to get a monitor that has one of those two certifications.

G-Sync can cost extra because it uses a hardware module inside the monitor. However, NVIDIA has certified plenty of monitors as G-Sync Compatible. This means that a monitor doesn't need that module to work with G-Sync. Many FreeSync monitors are G-Sync Compatible.

Console gamers can completely ignore G-Sync, as no console uses an NVIDIA GPU. The Xbox Series X and Series S support FreeSync because of their AMD hardware, while the PlayStation 5 and PS5 Pro support only HDMI 2.1 VRR.

Regardless of your hardware, VRR only works with the right cable. Make sure you're using a cable with at least DisplayPort 1.2a. If you're using HDMI, some HDMI 1.4 and 2.0 connections will support VRR, but the most robust support comes with HDMI 2.1 onward.

PinkPantheress Channeled a High-Fashion Present at the 2026 CFDA Awards

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Last night, the 2026 CFDA Awards in New York City proved yet again to be a star-studded affair: Everyone from Hailey Bieber and Zoë Kravitz to host Keke Palmer (all clad in gold!) turned out for the event, which celebrates American designers. And there was an additional star in the mix who shimmered in gold, too: British singer PinkPantheress, who hit the step and repeat in custom Coach.

PinkPantheress has been diving even deeper into the world of high fashion of late, so attending this year’s CFDAs felt like the perfect opportunity to further experiment with her own approach to red carpet dressing. “This year, my team and I have been able to establish a fun world and storyline through our styling of tour runs,” PinkPantheress tells Vogue. “Through these experiences, my love and understanding of fashion has grown exponentially. It’s really fun to dive into an art form with such an everyday necessity as fashion.”

Working with Coach designer Stuart Vevers, PinkPantheress wanted to lean into the night’s celebratory and upbeat feeling—so they collaborated on a striking look that, well, resembled a golden present. “I worked with Stuart on a look inspired by the spring 2027 runway,” says the star. “There was little we changed, as it already captured exactly the vibe we were going for. It has the effortless cool of ’90s dressing, relaxed, but entirely my own.”

The golden lamé bra top and floor-length skirt were punctuated with a big black tulle bow at the back. “The oversized bow adds a playful, celebratory touch, while their signature detailing brings in a more refined element,” says PinkPantheress. “It feels personal and a little unexpected, with a sensibility that feels true to my style.” She also accessorized with a custom gold Tabby 26 bag by the label. “It brought everything together,” she says.

As for some of the musician’s favorite moments of the night? That would be hanging out with Vevers and running into some old friends. “I bumped into my friend Keith [Herron], who runs the brand Advisry, and had a great chinwag with him,” she says.

Post-CFDAs, PinkPantheress is taking a little ‘her time’—so the red carpet serves (and any new music prospects) will be on a momentary pause. “I’ve been dabbling in some artistic pursuits outside music, which have been super rewarding and taking the majority of my time,” says PinkPantheress. “But now that we’re in November, I’m excited for some at-home movie nights and staying inside for a while. Really excited to take a hefty little break.”

Below, see how PinkPantheress got ready for the big night.

Range Rover Evoque and Velar Won’t Live Past 2027: Report

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

But don’t worry if you’re not in the upper 1%; JLR supposedly has plans to keep affordable Land Rover models in the mix.

The Range Rover Velar and Evoque could soon be facing the axe, as a new report from Autocar contends that both of Range Rover’s smaller, entry-level models won’t see a new generation.

The report cites Martin Limpert, Range Rover’s global managing director, as saying both the Evoque and Velar will be produced through 2027, but not beyond. The current generation Evoque was revealed all the way back in 2018 and went through a mid-cycle refresh for the 2024 model year. Meanwhile, the Velar is even older, having been revealed in 2017. It too was treated to a mid-cycle refresh for 2024.

The elimination of both the Evoque and Velar would significantly increase the cost barrier to entry for a car with the Range Rover badge on it. It would also leave the “Range Rover” brand with only a few models—those being the traditional Range Rover, Range Rover Sport, and incoming Range Rover GT.

But that might not be such a terrible thing. Instead of targeting the lower end of the market with Range Rover-branded models, JLR appears ready to use both its Defender and Discovery brands for those endeavors. Reports of a smaller Defender model suggest it could easily take the place of the Evoque. Meanwhile, the Discovery already comes in around the same price as a Velar.

Both the Evoque and Velar launched as relatively controversial projects, but ultimately led to JLR increasing sales numbers. Applying the Range Rover badge to smaller and more affordable cars was a way to make the brand more accessible, and plenty of folks were happy to jump on the train. It even opened some strange doors, with the Range Rover Evoque Convertible being the weirdest of them all. When the Evoque and Velar finally exit, they’ll join the Discovery Sport that’s confirmed to end production this year.

(Road & Track reached out to Land Rover to confirm the report; we will update this story if we hear back.

misha kahn turns upholstery into painting and marble into fabric at friedman benda

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

the designer discusses his return to hands-on experimentation, where unexpected materials and processes reshape familiar domestic objects.

Misha Kahn‘s latest exhibition, All Intensive Purposes, brings a radical collection of furniture and sculpture to Friedman Benda, marking ten years of collaboration with the gallery. A sofa resembles a pile of painted shapes, and a marble console seems to sag beneath the drapes of its own fabric-like surface.

The works reveal Kahn’s fascination with material experimentation and the unpredictable ways an idea can develop through the process of making. At the show’s opening, designboom spoke with the artist and designer about the thinking behind these unusual objects and his return to more tactile methods of working.

Throughout the New York gallery, familiar objects take on unexpected qualities. Striped plywood returns to tree-like forms, copper is worked into the appearance of loosely knitted fabric. There’s even a ‘lazy river’ circulating through the space.

For much of his career, Misha Kahn has moved between physical craft and digital experimentation, producing furniture through everything from improvised molds to virtual reality. His earlier work often carried the exaggerated, gravity-defying appearance of objects conceived on a computer screen. This time, he describes a shift toward something more immediate.

‘I think, the past handful of years, I really was doing so much work conceptually, digitally,‘ the artist tells designboom. ‘I think this show was really sort of a studio move.‘ Working partly from his studio in the woods, he describes a spiritual shift toward something ‘really tactile,’ with collage and material experimentation becoming central to the new pieces.

The new upholstered pieces offer one of the clearest examples of Kahn’s current approach. Their rounded forms appear assembled from enormous painted fragments, with irregular cushions that overlap and separate. Kahn explains that the process began with a painted composition, which was subsequently translated into wool by textile studio JG SWITZER. The material was then brought back into the furniture-making process, with patterns used to reconstruct the original painted arrangement across the upholstered surfaces.

‘I’ve never gotten that control over the surface,‘ the artist says, explaining that conventional upholstery typically requires working within the limitations of existing fabrics. The new process allowed him to develop the form and its graphic treatment together.

The construction underneath is equally unconventional. ‘We make the frames in the studio, and they’re welded so that the shapes can really flow,‘ Kahn explains. ‘I find that the most satisfying part is sort of the air between the cushions, because we’re so used to seeing a continuous form.‘ Some of the cushions appear loosely attached, raising questions about which parts can actually be moved, while additional pillow elements extend the composition even further.

Perhaps the exhibition’s most unexpected addition is a miniature lazy river, a winding installation that circulates through the gallery alongside a large video projection. The idea came to Kahn with surprising simplicity. ‘I thought, I don’t know why, I think like the beach,‘ he recalls, laughing. ‘I was like, this show needs a lazy river.‘

The video presents an abstract sequence of actions in which materials are repeatedly manipulated and transformed, eventually producing a kind of juice. ‘I wanted to make this video, like, a really abstract version of what it feels like to make objects,‘ he explains, describing the experience of doing something, taking it apart, and putting it back together over and over again.

Kahn designed a series of plastic bottles to accompany the installation, using the familiar manufacturing process associated with commercial water bottles to produce unusual, doubled forms. The arrangement circles continuously, offering a physical interpretation of the exhibition’s title, All Intensive Purposes, which shifts the familiar expression toward the intensity of making, even when the destination remains uncertain.

Kahn has spent much of his career working somewhere between furniture and sculpture. His pieces maintain a recognizable connection to domestic objects, while their unusual proportions and complex surfaces complicate their intended use. In Throws of Passion, for instance, a substantial marble console is carved to resemble draped fabric, its folds appearing almost weightless despite the solidity of the stone.

Asked whether his practice has recently moved further toward sculpture or functional furniture, Kahn describes both as part of the same imagined environment. ‘I feel like I always just wanted to feel like it’s coming from one world,‘ he says. ‘Objects really communicate an idea because I think you understand them as, like, oh, this is what’s in the house, and this is what goes there.‘

He also acknowledges the freedom that comes with making objects beyond the expectations of furniture. ‘I have really enjoyed making sculpture because it just doesn’t have some of the same kind of requirements,‘ he explains. Still, the distinction appears of limited interest to him. ‘I always feel like it should have both. All of my favorite historical figures did both.‘

gallery: Friedman Benda | @friedman_benda

location: 515 West 26th Street, New York, NY

UiPath will charge per document for its new finance AI agents

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

UiPath’s ready-made procurement and invoice processing agents arrive in early 2027, with customers paying per document processed. The company showed them at its FUSION event in Dubai.

Mircea Giurcan of UiPath presents the Source-to-Pay session at UiPath FUSION in Dubai, 2 November 2026.

UiPath plans to charge companies for each document its new finance agents process, instead of selling them a software project. Mircea Giurcan, a senior transformation director at UiPath, set out the model on Monday. He spoke at UiPath FUSION, held at Dubai’s Museum of the Future.

The invoice processing and procurement parts of the UiPath Solution for Source-to-Pay will be available from early 2027, he said. Further finance modules, including order-to-cash, are due in mid-2027.

“Your accounts payable is not special. It shouldn’t be special. Still, an invoice is an invoice.” Mircea Giurcan, UiPath

Giurcan said UiPath’s most mature customers spend most of the effort in an automation project before anything runs. They pick a process, analyse its metrics, interview the staff who do it and build a business case. Many finance processes are the same across most customers, whatever their industry, UiPath said.

About a year ago, UiPath’s product team decided to turn standard finance work into a ready-made product. It has two sides. One guides employees as they buy goods and services, and the other handles the invoices that follow each order. Customers then pay the “price per item times items processed”, Giurcan said.

UiPath first announced agents for procurement in March, as the UiPath Solution for Purchase-to-Pay. That announcement gave neither a price nor a release date.

On the procurement side, Giurcan showed an employee who wanted to buy ten laptops for an event. An agent checked whether that person could request that equipment in that number. It also found who had to approve it and which vendor usually supplies the item.

It then flagged that the quoted price was higher than in past orders. The agent drafted an email to the distributor’s sales representative, citing the requisition number, and paused the order until a reply came. A buyer would spend about half an hour on that, Giurcan said.

On the invoice side, the agents pick up invoices however they arrive, check them and post them into the customer’s ERP system.

Giurcan added that Cartographer, the process-mapping agent UiPath launched in September, will cut the interviews needed to map a process. At the same event, chief executive Daniel Dines said people must steer AI. UiPath’s finance push follows its WorkFusion acquisition in February. Its AI-agent pivot won over investors in July.

XBOX Confirms What Everyone Knew About The Elder Scrolls 6

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Politics  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Elder Scrolls 6 has been the talk of the town for months, with whispers of announcements and gameplay showcases constantly circulating. Bethesda has been resting on its laurels for quite a while now, using adaptations and remakes to buy time while they work on new entries in both of their flagship franchises.

Wuthering Waves is one of the biggest Gachas on the planet, and they do far better than most at answering player complaints. Let's go over exactly how!

Still partnered with and owned by XBOX, the title of Bethesda’s newest fantasy epic has been a bit of an open secret, one finally confirmed by the biggest names at XBOX. In a Twitter post, Asha Sharma herself announced the title of Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel. Continuing the theme of subtitles marking the game’s major locale, it’s easy to assume ES6 will take place in the capital of the same name.

Elder Scrolls 6 won’t be the first time series long players have entered the opulent city of Sentinel, as the desert-flanked capital also appeared in Elder Scrolls Online, and as far back as the original The Elder Scrolls: Arena.

Sentinel has a rich history for both players and the setting itself, standing apart as a city flanked by opposites, in both politics and environment. The split between ocean and desert does wonders to make it feel truly fantastical, while Bethesda has taken care to use real-world analogues to make the title location feel genuine.

After Skyrim’s focus on ice-cold mountains and Norse-themed culture, the Redguard housing Sentinel is set to be a major visual shift, one primed to breathe new life into the franchise. Bethesda is kind of playing with fire here, as many are expecting Sentinel to be an absolute blockbuster rivaling Skyrim’s universal acclaim.

With a new location to explore, one deeply ingrained in the lore and worldbuilding of The Elder Scrolls as a whole, Sentinel has the potential to be Bethesda’s biggest game ever, especially since the new Fallout is being handled by the team at Obsidian. With all their cards on the table, Bethesda are remaining remarkably tight-lipped, only unveiling the proper subtitle on their time, even when most already knew it.

There’s a respect to be paid for the adherence to their own schedule, but they’ll need to keep a tight ship. If Grand Theft Auto 6 has been any indication, there are individuals willing to hard focus on leaking major titles if the opportunity presents itself, and if proper gameplay or story spoilers manage to leak, I don’t think Bethesda will be able to stick their heads in the sand and wait it out.

For many, despite its age, Skyrim is still the archetypal fantasy RPG, a showcase of everything the general public wants out of the subgenre. Magic, monsters, and wild opportunities to help define both the setting and player character as an individual are all expectations they’ll need to meet, primed with experience and feedback from their latest attempts.

Elder Scrolls 6 won’t be the first time series long players have entered the opulent city of Sentinel, as the desert-flanked capital also appeared in Elder Scrolls Online, and as far back as the original The Elder Scrolls: Arena.

After the crash and burn of their most recent RPG attempt, Starfield, Bethesda needs to redeem themselves in the eyes of the general public, even as remasters like Oblivion and drops like Doom: The Dark Ages buy them time. It’s so exciting to think about one of the titans of the RPG genre returning to form, but it’s a little hard to be blindly optimistic.

Bethesda's hopes for the next generation are all riding off the back of Elder Scrolls Sentinel. Maybe a return to such an established region will give them the flow state they need to win back the gaming public while putting out their best work. The devil you know, after all.

After ignoring the response to a poll they set up, VR Chat is getting review-bombed by some of its most loyal players.

Journey Now Say Their Farewell Tour Isn’t the End. What Could That Mean?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

On Nov. 28, Journey will bring their “Final Frontier” farewell tour to a close with a homecoming show at San Francisco’s Chase Center. “It just feels like a good time to get off the train,” keyboardist Jonathan Cain said shortly before the tour began. “You’ve got to at some point say enough’s enough. It’s a grind, man. I just feel like this tour is a beautiful closing chapter…It’s time to move on.”

The first indication that the goodbye in San Francisco wouldn’t truly be a farewell came in September, when their name appeared on the 2027 Stagecoach festival lineup. But this was a makeup date, since their planned Stagecoach performance this year was rained out. It seemed like Stagecoach was possibly going to be a quickie encore show that truly marked the end of the band.

But earlier this week, Journey founder Neal Schon shared a message on Facebook that made it very clear their future plans involved a lot more than Stagecoach. “After completing 110 shows in 2026, we’ve felt something powerful happen within Journey,” he wrote. “Playing two and a half hours each night in our ‘Evening with Journey’ shows—the longest shows we’ve ever played—has brought us closer together, both musically and personally. Our fans have touched our hearts and helped pull us together. Your love, energy and passion for this music have reminded us how much we still share and how much this journey means to all of us. What began as ‘The Final Frontier’ now feels more like ‘The Long Goodbye.’ We’re letting our hearts—and the connection we feel with you every night—help guide what comes next.”

And despite numerous unambiguous statements that he was stepping aside after this tour, Cain is also seemingly changing his mind. “I’m overwhelmed with the turnout on this tour,” he recently told Ultimate Classic Rock. “It’s so strong. The fans have just been terrific. I’ve always looked at it like, well, you’ve got to know when it’s time to walk away, but just judging from this turnout, maybe we think about it. Right now we’re focusing on this tour. We’ve played everywhere we can play, and we’ve really been beating the bushes. I think it’s time for a little bit of a rest, so we’re gonna leave it here and see what happens.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Journey have been through more unpredictable twists and turns than just about any other rock group in history. It’s difficult to know exactly what’s going to happen in the future, but here are seven possible scenarios — and one that isn’t happening no matter how desperately some of you might wish to see it.

The simple and obvious move is to shrug off the whole “farewell tour” thing and just keep touring with this exact lineup. That would be the move from the playbook of the Who, Kiss, Mötley Crüe, Ozzy Osbourne, Tina Turner, Slayer, and just about every other act in music history that staged farewell tours. Some of these artists genuinely intended to step aside before changing their minds years later, and others simply saw a reliable gimmick to dramatically increase ticket sales and had no actual intention of retiring. In either case, there’s no rock & roll police force to stop groups from booking additional tours after their farewell runs end. Some jilted fans might stay home in the future, but the vast majority will come back.

One day before Journey kicked off their supposed farewell tour, Journey frontman Arnel Pineda told Rolling Stone that he nearly left the band prior to this tour. “Back in 2024, I said to them, ‘If you’re planning to do a farewell tour, you better tell me, because my issues and my personal problems are getting more intense, and I don’t know if I want to go with you,'” he said. “I said, ‘I want you to discuss the schedule with me.’ It is what it is now…. But then, I was really not happy with how they scheduled this tour. My body has changed. I can’t take the cold weather anymore.” Related Content Journey Drummer Deen Castronovo Suffers Medical Emergency During Tulsa Concert Journey Brought Out Hugo Valenti for a Second Time — And This Time He Nailed It Watch Longtime Steve Perry Impersonator Hugo Valenti Finally Guest With Journey Journey Add 40 Additional 2026 Dates to Final Frontier Farewell Tour

Schon confirmed his account. “It’s been very confusing,” he said. “He sent a lot of messages that he was overwhelmed with his personal life and didn’t know if he could do it. But we all signed contracts…Whatever goes down, I’m ready to plow through it, survive, and float to the top…I love Arnel. He’s been a total martyr, like a warrior.”

After the final bow in San Francisco, Pineda will likely have fulfilled the terms of his contract with concert promoters AEG. It’s certainly possible he feels better about sticking around and will sign on for additional tours. But it’s also possible he’s planning on heading back to the Philippines, never to return. In that case, they’ll need yet another new lead singer.

Back in 1998, singer Hugo Valenti — who bears a striking physical and vocal resemblance to Steve Perry — got a call from Sony A&R rep John Kalodner about possibly joining the band. But the idea was nixed before he could even audition. “I remember seeing him in a video, and he looked exactly like Perry, which I thought was really weird,” Schon said in 2003. “I said if we are going to get somebody with him looking exactly like Perry, I thought that was a little too weird!” Valenti went on to front the successful Journey tribute band Voyage, and now leads an entity called Hugo – The Ultimate Journey Tribute.

But in August, out of nowhere, Journey invited him onto their stage at two shows on their Japanese tour, where he sang “Faithfully” and “Keep on Runnin’.” He had some vocal problems on night one due to issues with his in-ear monitors, but sounded much better at the second show. Might this have been a test run for a future incarnation of Journey? (This may sound unlikely, but Robbie Krieger and Ray Manzarek initially rejected longtime Jim Morrison impersonator Dave Brock when they formed a new version of the Doors in 2002, but eventually made him a part of the band.)

Several lead singers have fronted Journey over the years, including Gregg Rolie, Robert Fleischman, Steve Perry, Steve Augeri, and Jeff Scott Soto. They reached the zenith of their popularity with Perry, and continuing without him initially seemed like a ludicrous proposition. But once they hired Arnel Pineda in 2007, the group was once again headlining arenas and even the occasional stadium. There’s no reason they can’t find another young, hungry singer to take Pineda’s place should this become necessary. (Valenti is 65 and perhaps not quite ready for the intense rigors of a long Journey tour.) They found Pineda on YouTube, and there must be at least one person on this planet capable of filling his shoes. They just have to start looking.

Prior to Steve Perry joining the band in 1977, original Santana lead singer Gregg Rolie provided the lead vocals from behind a keyboard. And on this tour, Pineda steps to the side quite a few times to grab a breath and allow Cain, drummer Deen Castronovo, and keyboardist Jason Derlatka a chance to sing a song or two. They all do remarkable jobs, especially Castronovo. It’s slightly hard to imagine, and it might take them out of arenas and into smaller venues, but they could continue to play Journey shows with Cain, Castronovo, and Derlatka handling all the vocals.

Back in 2019, when Journey were on a brief break, Schon hit the club circuit with a new group he called Journey Through Time. It included Rolie and Castronovo, and the shows dug deep into the Journey catalog and resurrected songs they hadn’t touched in decades. This thrilled hardcore Journey fans, but enraged the other members of the group. They felt the name was simply too close to Journey. That’s why Journey Through Time lasted for just a matter of days.

But Schon recently trademarked the name Journey Beyond, and he’s hinted that’s the name of his next project. If Journey indeed decides to call it a day, Cain actually retires, and Schon doesn’t face another legal hurdle over the name, it’s quite possible the future of Journey is Journey Beyond. It’s unclear who would be in such a group, but OG fans would love to see Rolie back. It would bring the saga full circle.

This one feels the least likely, but it is worth considering the possibility that Journey have a change of heart about their change of heart and never play again in any form after Stagecoach. But who are we kidding? That’s not going to happen. It’s more likely they’ll tour until Neal Schon is too old and feeble to hold a guitar, and then they’ll likely take a page from the Foreigner playbook and just license the name out to a bunch of randos. As long as people are willing to go out and see “Journey” play the hits, there will likely be some version of the group happy to take their money. Trending Stories Dane Cook’s Brother Robbed Him Blind. Now He’s Rebuilding His Life — And Gunning for a Comeback Andy Garcia, David Foster, Paul Anka Lead Kennedy Center Honors Yes, Matt Damon and David Letterman Were Background Actors on ‘The Gilded Age’ Season Premiere Britney Spears Says Kevin Federline and Jamie Lynn Spears Made Her ‘Feel Like a Slave’

We live in a world of vast possibilities. The fact that any of us even exist is the product of wildly unlikely circumstances, and yet here we all are. If you believe in the multiverse theory popularized by comic books and Rick and Morty, there are infinite versions of reality where anything that’s possible is actually happening. That includes worlds where hammerhead sharks have wings and fly, black olives are currency, and William “Refrigerator” Perry served six terms as president after single-handedly fighting off an alien invasion. But in none of these infinite worlds does Steve Perry return for a Journey reunion tour. A certain contingent of fans will never let the dream go, but it’s never, ever, ever happening. Everyone needs to accept this and move on with their lives.

Google says students in schools that ban AI won't have access to Search

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Gotta get those AI user numbers up somehow.

Google seems to have shoved as many AI features into as many of its products as it can over the last few years in an attempt to justify the tens of billions of dollars it has plowed into the technology. It appears to be making another attempt to bump up its AI user numbers by issuing an ultimatum to schools: enable AI features or students won't be able to use Search.

In one of several blog posts Google published Tuesday about its education efforts, it said it believes educators should have control over the use of AI in schools. "After listening to schools, teachers, leading researchers and policymakers, we are now updating our approach to controls for Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom and Google Search," Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager for education, wrote. "Access to these products will be entirely off for K-12 student accounts until school administrators explicitly turn them on." Google clarified to Engadget that  administrators will be able to enable these products individually, and that it's not an all-or-nothing deal.

The company plans to roll out these updated controls in the coming months as it walks back a change it made this summer. In August, Google enabled certain AI features by default in the Classroom learning platform. That shift prompted a backlash from educators, as The New York Times reported, leading Google to reverse course.

However, Phillips told the publication that "Search and AI are deeply integrated." If a school bans AI tools, students "will not have access to Search, and they'll see a message from their school that indicates it's not available."

Chromebooks and Google's education apps are widely used in schools, particularly in the US. If school districts don't play by Google's new rules, they may have to consider alternative search engines (and perhaps other devices and digital learning platforms) going forward.

Several jurisdictions have limited access to generative AI in schools. In September, New York City placed a one-year moratorium on the use of such tools in public schools for students through eighth grade. The Los Angeles public school system enacted a similar ban that same month. Last week, New York Governor Kathy Hochul signed a law that bans AI companies from offering certain chatbot features to minors.

In school districts and jurisdictions that do allow the use of AI, Google offers a tool called Guided Learning, which is available globally in Classroom starting today. Rather than giving students the answer to a question or activity assigned by their teacher, Guided Learning (which was already available in the Gemini app) breaks problems down into step-by-step explanations and offers interactive features like quizzes. Google suggested it's akin to one-on-one tutoring.

The company said it's adding Guided Learning tools to Search in student accounts as well, ostensibly as a measure to limit cheating. "When our systems understand a student is looking for certain types of homework help, Search will walk them through the topic or problem step by step," Phillips wrote. "For example, if a student pastes a complex physics problem or asks for help writing a book report in Search, they'll see prompts to help them think critically and work through the concepts, without going directly to the answer."

The company will test this approach in select schools in the US starting this month before a broader expansion. In the school version of Guided Learning, teachers will be able to access transcripts of chatbot interactions on student accounts.

North Korea denies role in $6 billion Binance hack after FBI accusation

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reuters, citing KCNA, reported the denial after the FBI blamed TraderTraitor for stealing more than $6 billion from Binance.

Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed US claims linking the country to the Binance hack as “absurd rhetoric,” Reuters reported on Nov. 2, citing the Korean Central News Agency, or KCNA. The denial disputes the US government's attribution of the theft of more than $6 billion from the cryptocurrency exchange.

The FBI had blamed North Korea's TraderTraitor operation in an Oct. 31 alert, placing the theft on or about Oct. 29. TraderTraitor is the bureau's name for the specific North Korean cyber activity it identified in the attack.

Blockchain intelligence firm TRM Labs estimated that approximately $6 billion left Binance wallets across ten blockchains, with most of the funds moving in about 20 minutes beginning at 20:05 UTC on Oct. 29. The firm's initial figures reflected observations through 00:30 UTC on Oct. 30.

TRM attributed the Binance hack to TraderTraitor with high confidence, citing on-chain and off-chain intelligence.

The firm also assessed that valid signed transfers across different blockchains pointed to a compromise of Binance's hot-wallet signing infrastructure. That would place the breach in the systems used to authorize outgoing wallet transactions. The explanation remains TRM's assessment of how the transfers were enabled. Its conclusion connects the ability to produce valid transfers on different networks with a potential weakness in the infrastructure used to sign those transactions.

Cat-sized foxes in Texas are in trouble. Big guard dogs could help.

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 3, 2026 5:10 PM EST

Two guard dogs could be protecting more than just the livestock grazing on the Rita Blanca National Grasslands in the Texas Panhandle. The working canines may be helping increase the odds of survival for the Lone Star State’s smallest fox.

The swift fox (Vulpes velox) is about the size of a house cat. While not federally listed as endangered, conservationists believe there are fewer than 250 left in Texas. They only occupy about 40 percent of their historic range, from Texas north to Alberta, Canada.

“The swift fox relies on the shortgrass prairie ecosystem once found across the Panhandle and into western Texas,” Haley Sloan, a doctoral student at Texas A&M, said in a profile. “Coyotes can take advantage of the cover provided by the taller vegetation that has grown into these areas, resulting in coyote predation being a leading cause of mortality in swift fox populations.”

Sloan is currently studying if livestock guardian dogs paired with a cattle herd can reduce swift fox mortality. The thought is that the dogs will act as a larger predator, and change the way the coyotes behave and use their habitat.

“The hope is that the dogs’ presence will deter coyotes, thereby reducing swift fox mortality and allowing the foxes to start using areas where the coyotes are being excluded,” Sloan said.

The dogs work in northwestern Texas’ Dallam County, the only known county in the state that still has a swift fox population. The swift fox’s small size and diet of insects, plants, and small rodents also means that they don’t pose any threats to cattle, sheep, and goats.

If incorporating these livestock guardian dogs with cattle herds alters the coyotes’ behavior, Sloan said that it offers two potential benefits. It could improve the chances of swift fox survival, while reducing the threat of livestock predation, especially during the vulnerable calving season.

To monitor the animals’ behavior, Sloan and the other researchers put GPS collars on coyotes and swift foxes ahead of this three-year-long project. The livestock guardian dogs were introduced with the cattle herd this summer. While it is too early to report any initial findings, the team has high hopes.

“I’m happy to report right now the dogs are doing great,” Sloan said. “The producer involved in the project hasn’t lost any calves to predation, and I have hope we’ll have similar positive outcomes for swift foxes in the vicinity of the herd.”

Android Auto 18.1 update rolling out with additional prep for light mode and redesign

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Google is rolling out Android Auto 18.1 to the masses and, while there are no major changes, the update continues to work on upcoming changes.

Following Android Auto 18.0’s release in mid-October, Google released v18.1 to the beta track in late October. As of this week, the update is now rolling out to stable users. As far as we’ve been able to tell thus far, there are no user-facing updates here, but there are a few things going on behind the scenes.

About APK Insight: In this “APK Insight” post, we’ve decompiled the latest version of an application that Google uploaded to the Play Store. When we decompile these files (called APKs, in the case of Android apps), we’re able to see various lines of code within that hint at possible future features. Keep in mind that Google may or may not ever ship these features, and our interpretation of what they are may be imperfect. We’ll try to enable those that are closer to being finished, however, to show you how they’ll look in case that they do ship. With that in mind, read on.

In a teardown of the latest Android Auto 18.1 update, we’ll start off by saying there’s nothing groundbreaking going on here. There’s a growing trend of making a really big deal out of any little Android Auto change or feature lately, but we’ll say right up front that there’s nothing major happening in this update.

Rather, Google continues to build out its redesign and its light mode, which at this point seems to be arriving with that redesign.

Strings that caught our eye in this update include assets specific to Samsung devices that include “dark_mode” and “light_mode” variants, though these seem to be applicable to non-Samsung devices as well. Android Auto already makes UI tweaks when you’re using a Samsung phone versus a Pixel or other Android device, so these assets are likely just being built out for the upcoming redesign. Specifically, we believe these are being prepared for Android Auto’s upcoming Quick Settings tiles which will presumably include some Samsung-specific icons or designs.

“Cielo,” the apparent codename for Android Auto’s widget-filled redesign, continues to appear in updates. In v18.1, we’re seeing mentions of map scaling and apparent animations used for the dashboard “slide in” and “slide out” effects. These aren’t a surprise as we saw them in early hands-on demos back in May.

There’s still no sign of exactly when Android Auto’s “Cielo” redesign may go live, but Google is clearly building everything out behind the scenes, implying it may come sooner than later.
